Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.75% on Tuesday 17-Nov to 650.7, its worst session since 20-Mar-2026 (the Market chart, 12 months), two sessions after its 12-month closing high of 664.1 on Friday 13-Nov. It is now 2.0% below that high and at its lowest close since 6-Nov. Over the dashboard's periods (the week is the 5 sessions from the 10-Nov close, the fortnight 10 sessions, the month 21 sessions; industry figures are each industry index's price move, the Market chart, 3 and 12 months): the index is down 1.2% in the week, up 0.8% over the fortnight, up 1.7% over the month, up 0.1% over three months and up 13.8% over twelve months.

  • The two sessions since the previous note's readings (13-Nov): the index fell 2.0% (16-Nov (0.3%), 17-Nov (1.7%)). Technology fell most ((3.2%), (2.7%) on 17-Nov alone), then Media (2.5%), Utilities (2.1%), Professional, business and consumer services (2.1%), Consumer discretionary (2.0%) and Industrials and capital goods (2.0%). Defence (+0.2%), Transportation (0.1%), Consumer staples (0.6%) and Telecoms (0.8%) held up best.
  • The week: Transportation +2.6% and Consumer staples +0.5% were the only industries up by more than a rounding; Defence (4.3%), Consumer discretionary (2.4%), Media (2.3%), Technology (2.2%) and Industrials and capital goods (1.9%) fell most.
  • The month (21 sessions): Healthcare +6.2%, Real assets +4.8%, Infrastructure +4.4% and Consumer staples +4.1% lead; Technology (5.1%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (4.3%), Defence (2.2%) and Industrials and capital goods (1.5%, 3.2 points behind the index) lag.

Theon is a broken trend in its fourteenth week: its SP closed at €26.88 on 17-Nov, 29.9% below its 14-Aug closing high (€38.32), and the week to 17-Nov undid the results bounce.

The three legs since the 29-Oct high: the industry led each one, and Theon amplified the falls more than the rises.

  • 29-Oct to 6-Nov, €30.64 to €26.74, (12.7%) in six sessions with no up day: on 5-Nov Theon fell 6.0% on 1.57 times its 50D average volume as Defence fell 3.0% and Rheinmetall 16.1% on 6.3 times, on Rheinmetall's results day. Hensoldt, Theon's closest electronics peer, rose 1.4% that day, on its own nine-month results (the 5-Nov date is from Börse Express, 9-Sep; no source read describes the results). On 6-Nov Theon fell 2.5%, Hensoldt 6.2% and Defence 1.2%. Reading: an industry leg that Theon followed with Rheinmetall rather than with Hensoldt.
  • 6 to 10-Nov, €26.74 to €28.62, +7.0%: on 9-Nov, Theon's third-quarter day, it rose 4.9% on 1.04 times, but Defence rose 3.9% and Hensoldt 6.9% the same day. The report beat: third-quarter revenue €134.1m (+40.4%) against Deutsche Bank's €130m, net debt 10% below its forecast, and the medium-term organic growth target raised to more than 20% a year from more than 15% (earnings review). On 10-Nov Theon rose 2.0% on 1.72 times after a 2.8% gap up, closing at 63% of the day's range; Deutsche Bank kept Buy and its €39 PT that day (earnings review). Reading: mostly the industry's rise; Theon's own beat added about one point.
  • 10 to 17-Nov, €28.62 to €26.88, (6.1%): on 11-Nov Theon fell 4.4% on 1.40 times, closing at 17% of the range, while Defence fell 1.6%, MilDef 6.0% and Rheinmetall 6.9% and the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 0.7%. The cause of the industry's fall on 11-Nov is not in the sources read. Theon then drifted on 0.63 to 1.14 times: its 2.8% gap up on 16-Nov closed at 2% of the range, and on 17-Nov it fell 1.2% as the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.7% and Defence 0.2%.
  • The split: the market explains (0.9) points of Theon's (6.1%) week; Defence fell 4.3%, so Theon moved about 1.4 times its industry.

The Investment Analyst reads the response to the results as negative: the market sold a clean beat because it prices peak leverage. The implications note (17-Nov) cites Deutsche Bank's projected FY2026 net debt of about €573m, 3.4 times EBITDA after the HGH purchase, against a covenant the case puts at 3.5 times, or 3.0 times if the revolving facility still carries the December 2025 level (the implications note: "3.5x or possibly 3.0x"), and a nine-month book-to-bill of 0.74 times against the case's 1.0 times. Would refute it: fourth-quarter orders of about €440m (Deutsche Bank) and the year-end net-debt print.

The MAs: Theon is below every MA, and its 100D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs have converged into one falling band 14.4% to 14.7% overhead.

  • Theon lost its absolute 200D MA for good on 24-Sep on 0.91 times; its 50D crossed below its 150D on 16-Oct and below its absolute 200D on 21-Oct.
  • The absolute 200D MA peaked at €31.67 on 16-Oct and has fallen 0.81% over 20 sessions to €31.39; the 150D (€31.51) and 100D (€31.46) are falling too; the 50D (€29.22) fell 7.7% over 20 sessions.
  • Each rally since the 8-Oct low died at the 50D: 29-Oct (€30.64, +7.0% on 1.70 times) regained it for one day. The 20D MA, regained on 23-Oct, was lost on 5-Nov.

Healthy Retest or broken trend? Theon against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: fails; it has fallen since 16-Oct, (0.81%) over 20 sessions.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D: passes, barely; €31.51 against €31.39, 0.4% apart and closing.
  • The 50D above the 150D and absolute 200D: fails; €29.22, below both since 16 and 21-Oct.
  • The SP above each: fails; 8.0% below the 50D, 14.7% below the 150D and 14.4% below the absolute 200D MA.
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 29.9% below €38.32.
  • Away from its low: passes for now, 14.0% above the 52W closing low (€23.58, 26-Nov-2025); once November 2025 and 1-Dec-2025 (€25.39) leave the window in early December, the low becomes €26.26 (30-Dec-2025), 2.3% below today's close.
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags, four of six present and one partly: the largest one-day fall since the advance began (5-Nov, (6.0%)) and the largest five-session fall ((12.1%) to 6-Nov), the 11-Nov fall two sessions after a beat-and-raise; wide swings (+7.0% on 29-Oct); a lower low (€26.74 on 6-Nov against €27.22 on 8-Oct); 40 down days against 26 up since 14-Aug; the 29-Oct breakout, on heavy rather than light volume, reversed on 1.57 times on 5-Nov. The sixth, tracking a rising MA, does not apply: no MA is rising.
  • Verdict: a broken trend, rated Stage 4 (declining) Plausible by the daily classification file on 17-Nov. Would settle it the other way: a close above €30.64 and then the €31.4 to €31.5 band on heavy volume. Would confirm it: a close below €26.74, the lowest since 2-Jan-2026 (€26.36).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Interestingly, OBV very positive over last month or so. But doesnt stop the SP from falling." On 13-Nov you wrote: "OBV is running at 125% though". Both readings are right, and together they say Theon's buyers arrive on a few bounce days that fail, while sellers work steadily on the rest.

  • What the 1.24 measures: the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's up/down volume ratio (1.24 on 17-Nov, over the 19 day-on-day comparisons to 17-Nov) is the average volume of an up day over the average volume of a down day: 158,006 shares against 127,332 (computed).
  • What it hides: Theon had 8 up days and 11 down days in that window, so total down-day volume exceeded total up-day volume; on totals the ratio is 0.90 (computed). Over the five sessions to 17-Nov the dashboard's ratio is 1.40, but on totals it is 0.30 (computed): one up day, 16-Nov, whose gap up was sold.
  • Reading: the buying is concentrated and real, but it has not held a single rebound: supply overhead, not accumulation.

Theon's five heavy-volume days in the last 25 sessions (1.5 times the 50D average or more): four were up days, and three of them were given back within one to five sessions; 15-Oct's gain was fully given back only on 5-Nov.

  • 15-Oct: +5.6% on 1.65 times (185,061 shares, €5.4m), closing at 98% of the range after a 1.2% gap down, as MilDef rose 11.2% on 3.7 times on its preliminary third quarter (Simply Wall St via Webull, 17-Oct).
  • 26-Oct: +4.0% on 2.07 times (233,420 shares, €7.0m), at 82% of the range, with Defence up 2.7%; reversed by 28-Oct's (4.6%).
  • 29-Oct: +7.0% on 1.70 times (198,986 shares, €6.1m), at 86% of the range, on Kongsberg Gruppen's third-quarter day (29-Oct, Sector Digest of 15-Oct; Kongsberg Gruppen +13.0% on 4.4 times); reversed from 30-Oct.
  • 5-Nov: (6.0%) on 1.57 times (191,234 shares, €5.2m), at 7% of the range, on Rheinmetall's results day.
  • 10-Nov: +2.0% on 1.72 times (218,451 shares, €6.3m), after a 2.8% gap up, at 63% of the range; reversed on 11-Nov's (4.4%) on 1.40 times.

Distribution and accumulation: three distribution days in 25 sessions (5, 6 and 11-Nov), against five accumulation days (15, 16, 26 and 29-Oct and 10-Nov). The count read 4 on 20-Oct, 3 on 3-Nov and 4 on 10-Nov: the October cluster aged out and a November one replaced it.

Across the whole decline, Theon's up-day volume has been the heaviest of its seven declines of 13% or more in 12 months, yet this decline is the longest.

  • From 14-Aug to 17-Nov the up/down ratio is 1.18 over 67 sessions (volume evidence script); the six earlier declines ran 0.66 to 1.06, over 7 to 34 sessions.
  • Hypothesis: holders sell into each rally the industry gives them. Against a liquidation: the 50D average volume rose only from 119,064 shares (2-Nov) to 127,816 (17-Nov), half October 2025's 253,438.

Volume at the lines: the 200-day line rule's exit was confirmed on 11-Nov on 1.40 times, a close at 17% of the range; the absolute 200D MA was lost for good on 24-Sep on 0.91 times.

Precedent: five of Theon's six completed declines in 12 months ended with a heavy-volume up day within ten sessions of the low that the SP built on (8-Dec-2025, +7.6% on 1.5 times; 7-Jan, +5.9% on 1.9 times; 18-Feb, +6.6% on 2.0 times; 1-Apr, +8.4% on 2.9 times; 2-Jul, +3.7% on 1.5 times). The exception, the 13-May low, turned on light volume (no day above 1.1 times in ten sessions) and still led to a 19.5% rise, so a turn without a heavy-volume day has a precedent. Since the 6-Nov low the candidate, 10-Nov, was reversed the next day on 1.40 times, faster than any precedent. Since then volume is light (0.63 to 1.14 times) and the daily range has narrowed from €1.74 to €0.84.

Mechanical-volume days: 18-Sep (an option expiry) sits in the 50D average; without it the ratio is unchanged at 1.24.

Relative price is at its 52W low and confirmed the November SP low; it did not confirm the 14-Aug SP high.

  • Relative price is 30.5% below its 52W high (7-Apr-2026) and 18.4% below its relative 200D MA; it fell 5.9% over four weeks and 27.1% over 13, and its 52W low was 13-Nov, 1.8% below today's level, while the SP's close held above 6-Nov's.
  • On 14-Aug, the SP's high, relative price was 9.5% below its own high: the non-confirmation came before the fall.

Peers' volume: on 5-Nov Hensoldt traded 2.4 times and Kongsberg Gruppen 1.5 times; on 10-Nov no cohort peer passed 1.4 times, so that day's volume was Theon's own; on 11-Nov MilDef traded 1.7 times and Rheinmetall 2.0 times.

Would confirm a turn: a close above €28.62 (10-Nov) on 1.5 times or more, in the top quarter of the range, then a pullback on light volume that holds above €26.74. Would confirm the decline continues: a close below €26.74 on more than 1.25 times the 50D average, a fourth distribution day.

3. RS

Theon's three RS readings are at or near their lowest stored, and the fall is a rotation out of Defence that Theon falls through, not Theon alone.

  • Stock RS: 10 on 17-Nov (9 at the 13-Nov reading), from 21 on 16-Oct, 32 at the 30-Oct month-end and 88 on 14-Aug; below 70 since 31-Aug and below 50 since 11-Sep.
  • Sector RS (Defence - Non-primes): 2, from 10 on 16-Oct, 21 at 30-Oct and 78 on 14-Aug; below 50 at every reading since 21-Aug except the 31-Aug month-end (57).
  • Industry RS (Defence): 0 at the 6 and 13-Nov readings and on 17-Nov, from 13 between 30-Sep and 4-Nov and 73 on 14-Aug; below 30 at all 11 Friday readings since 4-Sep. The Industry Digest's note of 13-Nov records Defence at the bottom for a fifth reading, with no source explaining why.

The order of the November leg: the sector first (Sector RS 21 to 7 on 4-Nov), the industry next (13 to 0 at the 6-Nov reading, the week of Rheinmetall's results), and Theon's Stock RS through both (32 to 12). The cohort fell with it: Hensoldt's Stock RS fell from 59 a month ago to 20 and Exosens' from 48 to 17 (peer table). Theon's RS fell while its EBITDA estimates rose (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)): the RS prices the group and the multiple, not the numbers.

Precedent: Industry RS had never read 0 in the stored history (from Oct-2025) before 6-Nov. When it last read 7 to 13 for several weeks (26-Jun to 31-Jul-2026), it reached 59 by 5-Aug, with Theon's Stock RS at 81 on 7-Aug, a week before the 14-Aug high. The more recent run at 13 (30-Sep to 4-Nov-2026, eight readings) brought no rotation back and ended at 0. The difference: Theon then sat above a rising absolute 200D MA; now it is 14.4% below a falling one, so a rotation back would start from a broken trend. Would confirm a rotation back: Industry RS of 30 or more at the Friday 20-Nov reading. Would confirm the fall continues: Stock RS below 9 with Sector RS at 0 or 1.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts keep raising Theon's revenue and EBITDA for FY2026 to FY2028 while FY2027 and FY2028 EPS stand still: the upgrades stop below EBITDA.

  • FY2027 (Theon's next full year): EBITDA €209.2m, up 4.4% in one month, 6.5% in three and 11.4% in six; revenue €760.2m, up 2.7%, 3.2% and 7.3%; EPS €1.66, (1.3%) in one and three months and (0.2%) in six.
  • FY2028: EBITDA up 3.4%, 5.5% and 10.9%; revenue up 2.7%, 3.7% and 7.4%; EPS €1.985, +0.1%, (1.0%) and +1.8%.
  • FY2026: EPS up 4.3% in one month (1.6% on 17-Nov) and 10.9% in three; revenue €601.6m, at the company's guidance of about €600m.
  • Trajectory: implied revenue growth is 26.4% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 17.1% to FY2028; EBITDA growth 29.5% and 17.8%; EPS growth only 4.5% and then 19.6%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 26.8% to 27.5% and 27.7%. The later year is rising no faster than the nearer one: the trajectory is being held, not raised.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Seeing Sales and EBITDA upgrades for 2026-27-28 but not for EPS; I think the Associate (Exosens) as per IA memo." Partly: the Exosens stake costs EPS through its funding, not through an associate loss.

  • The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo records the 9.8% Exosens stake (€268.7m) as "a supplier with no control and no income line", one of about €700m of commitments in 18 months, so it adds interest without adding earnings.
  • Deutsche Bank (10-Nov) expects interest on the €325m term loan for HGH to dilute adjusted EPS by about 5%, with FY2026 net debt near €573m.
  • The memo finds brokers split on interest and amortisation: UBS has the highest FY2027 revenue (€780m) and lowest EPS (€1.42), Oddo the reverse.

Link to your rule: on 3-Nov you wrote: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". On EPS, Theon's FY2027 consensus has not been upgraded over three or six months; on revenue and EBITDA it has. The SP did not follow the estimates down or up: it fell 26.7% in three months (from €36.68 on 20-Aug) while FY2027 EBITDA consensus rose 6.5%, so the fall is in the multiple. Deutsche Bank says consensus revenue is about 5% below management's targets (earnings review), but consensus did not move in the first week (implications note). Would confirm the upgrade case: FY2027 EPS rising after the HGH closing and the year-end net-debt print.

Theon's analysts raise the operating numbers and cut what they will pay for them: EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth is the highest stored, PT breadth has been negative for seven weeks.

  • EPS: (22%) (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 9), from (14%) at 30-Oct; negative or zero at every month-end since April.
  • PT: (10%) (2 raising, 3 cutting, of 10), from (25%) at 30-Oct and (20%) on 13-Nov; negative at every reading since the 30-Sep month-end ((11%)).
  • Buys: 8 of 9 (89%), with no Sells.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Recently a cut to PT. Why. PT breadth has been negative for last 6W. IA to investigate." The sources read name the logic but not this month's cutters: the cuts are about leverage and the land-defence multiple, not Theon's numbers.

  • The latest change: Theon's average PT fell 2.1% on 10-Nov, from €40.12 (4-Nov) to €39.31, as the PTs counted rose from 8 to 10 and the cuts from 2 to 3; it is €39.41 on 17-Nov, after one raise.
  • Who: Deutsche Bank (Sriram Krishnan) kept Buy and €39 on 10-Nov, set at a 15% discount to Exosens for the leverage and the idle Exosens stake (earnings review). The earnings review found no other broker response after 9-Nov; the brokers behind the 10-Nov change are not named in the sources read (the weekly AlphaSense report, which usually names them, was not read).
  • The one named cut and its logic: ING cut its PT to €38 from €42.50 on 15-Sep, on a lower 2027 EV/EBIT of 16.1 times, citing investor sensitivity to leverage, benchmarked to land-defence peers whose multiples fell from 21.6 to 17.2 times (Sector Digest, 29-Oct).
  • The Investment Analyst's investigation you asked for is listed for the main session.

Ratings follow valuation, not momentum, and show no downgrade. Buys rose from 5 to 7 at the November 2025 month-end as the SP fell 17.9% in the month. The one non-Buy appeared on 10-Nov, when the analysts counted rose from 7 to 9 with Buys at 8: a new rating joining, not a Buy leaving, though the implications note reads it as "a broker left Buy".

Theon's average PT is 46.6% above its SP, the widest gap since the November 2024 month-end (49.8%), before the SP tripled by May 2025.

  • The two other gaps above 35% in 24 months, at the November 2025 (36.6%) and June 2026 (35.7%) month-ends, came at lows that preceded rises of 34.5% and 28.4%.
  • Against it: the average PT, €39.41, is below its June 2026 month-end peak (€40.50) and close to April's €39.77, so the gap has widened only because the SP fell; in a de-rating a stale PT measures the multiple cut, not upside.
  • Test: two more PT raises without a cut would take PT breadth to positive, the first time since the August month-end (+10%).

Volex's sell-side and chart point opposite ways more sharply than at any time since January 2025: 7 of 7 analysts rate it Buy and the average PT is 44.3% above the SP, while Technical Momentum reads D.

  • Average PT: 736p on 17-Nov, from 729.4p on 10-Nov and 723.1p on 16-Oct. Jefferies kept Buy and 750p on 12-Nov (earnings review); Zeus's 730p and Berenberg's 750p date from 19 and 20-Oct.

The 44.3% PT-to-SP gap is the widest since January 2025, and its precedents point both ways. The gap was wider through the winter of 2024-25: 62.0% at the November 2024 month-end (at 289p), after which Volex fell a further 31% to its April 2025 low (199p on 4-Apr), and 67.3% at the March 2025 month-end (at 242p), after which it rose 44% to the August 2025 month-end (347p). Nearer this week's level, the October 2024 month-end read 41.2% (at 325p) and April 2025 40.7% (at 254p). A gap this wide says the sell-side has not changed its view of value, not when the selling stops. Test: whether a broker cuts its PT towards the SP in the second earnings review.

Net Upgrade Breadth: nobody is cutting Volex's estimates; the PT count is split and one broker away from half of a Sell Criterion.

  • EPS and revenue: +71% each (5 of 7 raising, none cutting) from 9-Nov, down from +100%: two raises rolled out of the 100-day window, not two cuts.
  • EBITDA: +86% (6 raising, none cutting) from 16-Nov, from +71%: one analyst raised after the results.

What the rules say for Volex now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most

  • The trend is broken, on your own tests: all six Stage 3 (topping) red flags show, two reclaims of the rising absolute 200D MA have failed (the second on 2.68x volume), and the closing lows keep falling (532p, 520p, 510p) (item 1 (SP and MAs)).
  • The selling is Volex's own and growing: seven distribution days in 25 sessions (four on 3-Nov), on days its peers traded light (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • The fundamentals point the other way more firmly than at any time since early 2025: FY2027 EPS +4.3% since 15-Oct, nobody cutting estimates, 7 of 7 Buys, the average PT 44.3% above the SP. This is the pattern you named on 10-Nov: "The "bear market" is positive fundamentals yet falling SP; it is loud and clear and true here". The rules sell on price, and the price has not turned.

What the rules say today

  • The 200-day line rule (the whole holding, four Core tranches): exit confirmed Fri 13-Nov, a close of 523p at or below the stop-loss (then 525.9p); not sold. Today: absolute 200D MA 560.2p, stop-loss 526.6p, close 510p (3.2% below the stop-loss). The whole holding sells at the Tue 24-Nov session.

4. Consensus by named FY

The cuts to Xvivo's FY2027 and FY2028 have resumed: SB1 Markets' initiation on 16-Nov took FY2027 consensus EPS down a further 4.9% in one day, after three weeks without change. FY2027 EPS stood at SEK 5.24 from 27-Oct, edged up to SEK 5.31 on 12-Nov (one analyst raising), and fell to SEK 5.05 in the 17-Nov reading; FY2028 EPS fell 4.3% the same day, to SEK 8.37.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS SEK 3.72, up 6.3% over 1 month and 5.7% over 3, the third-quarter beat; revenue SEK 988.5m, (0.5%) over 1 month; EBITDA SEK 202.7m, (1.8%) over 1 and 3 months, (10.0%) over 6.
  • FY2027 (year to Dec-27): EPS (16.4%) over 1 month, (17.3%) over 3 and (23.6%) over 6; revenue SEK 1,198m, (4.6%) over 1 and 3 months; EBITDA SEK 298.2m, (12.6%) over 1 month and (18.1%) over 6.
  • FY2028 (year to Dec-28): EPS (11.6%) over 1 month and (16.4%) over 6; revenue SEK 1,578m, (1.9%) over 1 month; EBITDA SEK 479.7m, (6.1%) over 1 month.

Analysts are cutting the nearer year hardest and pushing the earnings onto FY2028, the year of the United States heart launch. Implied EPS growth is 36% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 66% from FY2027 to FY2028. The implied EBITDA margin (EBITDA against revenue) is 20.5% for FY2026, 24.9% for FY2027 and 30.4% for FY2028; for FY2027 it has fallen from 27.3% at the July month-end, and it sits more than five points below the company's kept 2027 target of an EBITDA margin above 30% (Geography Digest, 4-Nov). FY2027 EBITDA is being cut almost three times as hard as revenue ((12.6%) against (4.6%) over 1 month), which the earnings review of 22-Oct explained by Services' (19%) gross margin.

What SB1 Markets' numbers imply, on my calculation from the change in the consensus average on 17-Nov (assuming the other analysts did not change that day, which the unchanged counts of raises and cuts support): FY2027 EPS of about SEK 3.50, FY2027 revenue of about SEK 1,179m, FY2027 EBITDA of about SEK 277m and a PT of about SEK 180, the PT that Finwire reports. SB1 Markets' FY2027 EPS is below the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's own SEK 3.82 (26-Oct), which expected consensus to fall toward SEK 4.0 to 4.5 by the third-quarter 2027 report.

The peer table on the page shows the levels; the pattern is that Xvivo has fallen while its peers' estimates and Stock RS rose, and this week the peers' momentum stalled without Xvivo gaining.

The four peer checks, cohort by cohort:

Verdict: neither a Healthy Retest nor a broken trend, because there is no Stage 2 (advancing) trend to Retest. The daily classification file of 17-Nov gives no Stage 2 (advancing) rating (1 of 4 groups) and no Stage 3 (topping) rating; as new capital D'Ieteren now fits no classification (Inflecting / S1→S2 Probing Bet on 13-Nov). The pullback is orderly, light and contracting. The reading that settles it: a close above about €176.0 ends the stop-loss test; a close below €170.4 would make the first lower low since March.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

D'Ieteren's pullback from its 4-Nov high has run on light, falling volume, and the first session of above-average volume since 6-Nov was a buying day: on Tue 17-Nov D'Ieteren closed in the top of its range while its market fell. The 18-Sep option-expiry and index-review day is the only mechanical-volume day in the 50D average; the share-volume ratios below leave it out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's in brackets.

  • 17-Nov: 65,451 shares (€11.4m), 1.04x the 50D average (1.01x), the most since 6-Nov, on the day the SP reversed from below its 100D, absolute 200D and 150D MAs to close at 0.84 of its range (item 1 (SP and MAs)).
  • 11 to 16-Nov, the drift: four sessions on 0.60x to 0.75x (0.58x to 0.73x), €6.5m to €8.1m a day, each closing in the bottom half of its range.
  • The turn, 5-Nov: 91,921 shares (€16.8m), 1.49x (1.46x), a distribution day, is still the pullback's only day of supply.

In the last 25 sessions one day passed 1.5x on share volume, 15-Oct (1.87x (1.81x), 102,590 shares, €17.7m), and it closed at the day's low while its industry rose 1.6%: the script calls it accumulation because the close was up 0.1%, but the close at the low reads as supply. The script's distribution days are 14-Oct (leaving the window on 18-Nov) and 5-Nov; the dashboard counts 1, from 3 at 20-Oct: no cluster of selling.

Up-day against down-day volume still favours the sellers over the month, while the five sessions read differently on the two bases.

  • 20 sessions: 0.87 (dashboard, the same without mechanical-volume days), with 11 down days against 8 up.
  • 5 sessions: the dashboard reads 1.22; on close-on-close changes I count 0.51 (one up day, 17-Nov, against three down days). The two bases disagree; the data question is in the hand-back.
  • Since the 4-Nov high: 0.30. The whole move from the 24-Sep low: 1.08, with 4 heavy up days and none down, against 1.61 for the March to April advance and 1.02 for June to August. 20D volume stands at 0.87x the 50D average (dashboard).

At its MAs: D'Ieteren closed below the 20D MA, tranche 1's line, on 13-Nov at 0.61x, which fits an orderly Retest and also a lack of buyers; 17-Nov is the first session of the pullback where volume rose as the SP reached a line and turned up from it.

Precedent: a delayed volume signal has not been a bad sign for D'Ieteren. The 25-Nov-2025 and 30-Mar-2026 lows drew a heavy up day the next session; the 24-Sep low drew its first eight sessions later (6-Oct, 1.6x); the 8-Jun low drew none in ten sessions and still led to a 16.0% advance.

Relative price rose 1.5% on 17-Nov to 96.20, still 3.0% below its falling relative 200D MA (99.18) and 18.1% below its 52W high (2-Feb). Its swing highs since August read 101.1 (5-Aug), 98.85 (4-Sep), 98.10 (22-Oct) and 99.45 (5-Nov): the 5-Nov high was above September's and October's but below August's, and it stopped short of the relative 200D MA (about 99.97 that day). Relative price has stayed below its relative 200D MA since 6-Aug.

4. Consensus by named FY

The first rise in D'Ieteren's EPS consensus since 15-Oct came in the data file's reading of 16-Nov, and first traded on 17-Nov: EPS and EBITDA up 1.4% to 2.0% for every named FY, revenue trimmed, so analysts raised margins rather than sales. The data file's daily consensus series records the change on 16-Nov and none on 17-Nov; the key-days table shows it at 17-Nov, the first session after it.

  • EPS (17-Nov; then over 1, 3 and 12 months): FY2026 (year to Dec-26) €12.93, +1.4%; (1.1%), (3.5%), (12.6%). FY2027 €15.32, +1.5%; (0.2%), (2.0%), (11.1%). FY2028 €17.69, +1.8%; (0.2%), (2.0%), (5.6%).
  • EBITDA: FY2026 €639.1m (+2.0%), FY2027 €697.0m (+1.5%), FY2028 €736.9m (+1.9%); each still 1.5% to 4.0% below three months ago.
  • Revenue: FY2026 €7,955m, FY2027 €8,254m, FY2028 €8,494m, each (0.2%) to (0.3%) on 17-Nov and (1.8%) to (2.6%) over 3 months.

The trajectory is unchanged: the later years rose about as fast as the nearer one, so analysts lifted the level, not the slope.

  • Implied EPS growth: FY2026 to FY2027 18.5% (18.3% before the change); FY2027 to FY2028 15.4% (15.2%).
  • Implied EBITDA margin: FY2026 8.0% (7.9% before), FY2027 8.4% (8.3%), FY2028 8.7% (8.5%). Three months ago FY2026 read 8.2% and FY2027 8.5%: the 17-Nov raise recovers about half the margin cut since the 9-Sep results.

Hypothesis: one or two analysts moved their models towards Belron's margins or D'Ieteren Automotive's cost cuts (344 roles in the transformation plan, earnings review of 9-Sep), raising EBITDA on lower revenue. Against it: the same update cut PTs (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)); the brokers and their reasons are not in the sources read.

The SP moved with the estimates: 17-Nov, the first session after the raise recorded on 16-Nov (a day D'Ieteren fell 0.6%), was D'Ieteren's best relative day since 4-Nov (relative price +1.5%), as the SP moved with the two largest cuts (10-Sep, (3.4%) on 4.4x, the cut recorded that day; 27-Oct, (1.8%), the session after the cut recorded on 26-Oct). Test: whether the raise spreads to more analysts by the 30-Nov month-end.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's ratings still carry no signal: all 14 analysts rate it Buy at 17-Nov (7 covering in late 2024), through the 13.2% fall to 24-Sep and every estimate cut.

The 17-Nov update split the breadth readings: EBITDA and EPS breadth improved, revenue and PT breadth worsened. Each changed by one or two analysts against 13-Nov.

  • SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth: +14% (4 raising, 2 cutting, of 14), from +29% at 13-Nov, +36% at the October month-end and +46% at September's: the third consecutive narrowing.
  • SS Sales Net Upgrade Breadth: (67%) (6 of 9 cutting, none raising), from (56%); the SS Sales reading of the Net Upgrade Breadth Sell Criterion, now as weak as at the September month-end.
  • EBITDA: (25%) (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 8), from (62%). EPS: (14%) (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 14), from (21%).
  • Average PT: €233.9, (0.4%) from €234.7 (29-Oct to 13-Nov) and (2.3%) from €239.5 (24-Sep to at least 5-Oct); it stands 33.7% above the SP, against 38.1% on 27-Oct and 30.8% at the August month-end.

Who is cutting, and why: the brokers who cut revenue and PT on 16 or 17-Nov are not named in the sources read (no AlphaSense report this run). Earlier: Deutsche Bank cut its profit before tax forecast by 1% after the 9-Sep results for the drag from D'Ieteren Automotive (earnings review); Jefferies warned that D'Ieteren's PT assumes a 30% holding discount unless a Belron listing brings substantial share buybacks (key-questions research filed 31-Oct).

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Upgrade breadth is very negative; maybe due to accounting conventions; IA to check." The check is not yet filed. The 17-Nov update sharpens the pattern: revenue breadth (67%) while EBITDA breadth moved to (25%), revenue cut while margins rise. That fits real cuts to D'Ieteren Automotive's sales with a richer group mix, more than an accounting convention.

Precedent: PT breadth narrowed the same way in spring. From +50% (February) to +30% and +18% at the March and April month-ends, with revenue breadth (25%) and (60%), as most of the 20.9% rally to 17-Apr was given back by 8-Jun (a 14.6% fall to €158.90, still above the 30-Mar low of €153.86); September's +46% to today's +14% is the same shape. Test: a PT cut without Belron news, or PT breadth below zero at 30-Nov, which with Stock RS below 50 would also meet the Net Upgrade Breadth Sell Criterion on its PT leg (a reading of exactly zero does not).

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's seven, as in the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's table.

D'Ieteren's sector is still split, and this week D'Ieteren held up with the stronger half while Auto1 kept falling sharply. In the week to 17-Nov Auto1 fell 14.9%, Sixt 3.9%, Ayvens 3.0%, Bilia 1.9%, ZIGUP 1.7% and D'Ieteren 1.2%, while Inchcape rose 0.4% and Inter Cars 2.8%.

Your four peer checks, for the week to 17-Nov:

  • Sell-side estimates: the 3-month EPS revisions still split the sector: Inter Cars +9%, Bilia +1%, Sixt and ZIGUP 0%; D'Ieteren (2%), Ayvens (4%), Inchcape (9%), Auto1 (11%). D'Ieteren's 17-Nov raise (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)) moved it from the car retailers' side towards the middle.

Last week's ranked-first hypothesis, that D'Ieteren trades with its car-retail half on bad days, failed its test this week. The 13-Nov note's test was the next day Auto1 or Inchcape fell 4% or more without Belron news, with a D'Ieteren fall of 2% or more confirming. The first such day after that note, 17-Nov, refuted it: Auto1 fell 4.7% and D'Ieteren rose 0.2%. The 13-Nov readings already held one day each way: on 10-Nov Auto1 fell 4.9% and D'Ieteren 2.5% (for it), and on 11-Nov Auto1 fell 5.3% on 1.9x and D'Ieteren 0.6% (against it). Revised reading: D'Ieteren's 6 and 10-Nov falls coincided with Auto1's, but D'Ieteren has not followed Auto1 down since; it trades more like Sixt, its most correlated stock (average correlation 0.30, moderate), and its industry.

Who turned first: Inter Cars, falling 4.2% on 5-Nov, the day D'Ieteren reversed, but it has regained 3.4% since 6-Nov; Auto1 turned on 6-Nov and has not stopped. Titan, the Belgian stock most correlated with D'Ieteren outside the sector (0.19, weak), has fallen 8.7% since 4-Nov, so Belgium does not explain D'Ieteren's hold.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "InterCars has strong SS momentum; maybe more interesting than D'Ieteren. Investigate". The week adds to the comparison: Inter Cars rose 2.8% as the sector fell, with 3-month EPS revisions of +9% and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +20%, against D'Ieteren's (67%), and it sits inside the same "older cars, more repairs" story as Belron (Sector Digest, 10-Nov).

What the peers imply for D'Ieteren's sell tests: with Auto1 (Stock RS 2), Inchcape (6) and Ayvens (20) at the bottom, Sector RS needs Sixt, Bilia, ZIGUP and Inter Cars to rise for it to clear 50 by 30-Nov; D'Ieteren's own move matters little to that test. What would change it: Auto1 stabilising, which would stop it dragging the sector reading lower each week.

  • (1) Part of 13-Nov's one-day flow unwinding, then the market's fall. For: Avanza's lead over Nordnet since the 12-Nov close shrank from 3.3 points to 1.4 (Avanza +0.5%, Nordnet (0.9%)), most of it on 16-Nov, a day Nordnet rose. Against: no news explains 13-Nov in the sources read.
  • (2) Sellers waiting at the 400 to 408 zone where the August advance stalled. For: both closes sat in the bottom third of the range. Against: neither day reached 1.5 times the 50D average (item 2 (Volume)).
  • Test, the week to 20-Nov: a close below the 50D MA (375.6) on heavy volume favours (2); a quiet hold above it favours (1).

The MAs: Avanza was rejected at its flat 100D MA for the second time in a week, and its falling 50D MA is about to cross below its rising 150D.

  • The 100D (384.76): flat ((0.18%) over 20 sessions); Avanza closed above it on 13-Nov and back below it on 17-Nov, after touching it and closing at the low on 10-Nov.
  • Rising: the absolute 200D MA (364.17, +1.04% over 20 sessions) and the 150D (374.00, +0.61%); the SP is 4.9% and 2.1% above them.
  • Falling: the 50D (375.58, (3.33%) over 20 sessions), now only 0.4% above the 150D. On my arithmetic, at a flat SP of 382.0 the 50D crosses below the 150D in six sessions, about Wed 25-Nov, as the mid-September closes of 385 to 393 (9 to 16-Sep) leave its window; that would fail one of your trend-health tests with no further fall in the SP.
  • The earlier turns: from September to 19-Oct the SP broke the 50D (2-Sep), 100D (24-Sep), 150D (16-Oct) and absolute 200D (19-Oct) in that order, and regained the absolute 200D (4-Nov), the 150D and 50D together (9-Nov) and the 100D (13-Nov); in May, the best precedent, the regained 150D and absolute 200D held and the SP ran 26.8%.

Healthy Retest or broken trend? Avanza against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes, +1.04% over 20 sessions and +3.8% over 63 (350.87 to 364.17).
  • The 150D above the 200D: passes, 374.00 against 364.17.

  • Away from its low: fails narrowly, 23.0% above the 52W closing low of 310.48 (24-Feb), short of the 30% the Stage 2 (advancing) test uses.

    • The largest one-day and one-week falls since the advance began (13-May): present, from October ((2.8%) on 16 and 19-Oct; (7.8%) over five sessions), after the third-quarter miss, not good news.

    • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: not yet tested; the test comes at the rising 150D (374.0).
  • Verdict: a pullback inside the fifth base, neither a broken trend nor yet a Healthy Retest: the trend tests mostly pass, the October flags (the largest falls and the lower lows) stand, and November has added none; the daily classification file still rates Avanza's Stage 3 (topping) Plausible on 17-Nov, the counter-case to a Healthy Retest. 4. Consensus by named FY

Avanza's consensus edged up in the week while the SP fell: FY2026 EPS +0.1% to SEK 19.38 and FY2027 EPS +0.2% to SEK 21.13 since 13-Nov, FY2028 unchanged at SEK 23.24; the one-month picture is still a trim to FY2026 and a hold on the later years.

  • FY2026: EPS (0.8%) over one month, +0.1% over three, +6.7% over six; revenue SEK 5,197m, (1.0%) over one month and (0.5%) over three.
  • FY2027: EPS +0.3%, +2.0% and +7.8%; revenue SEK 5,734m, (0.2%) and +1.0%.
  • FY2028: EPS +1.1%, +2.6% and +8.5%; revenue SEK 6,315m, +0.6% and +1.7%.
  • Avanza, a bank, has no EBITDA consensus.

The later year is rising as fast as the nearer one: analysts are keeping the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth is 9.0% from FY2026 to FY2027 on revenue growth of 10.3%, and 10.0% from FY2027 to FY2028 on 10.1%: FY2027 carries a small margin squeeze (the Danish entry's SEK 120 to 150 million, earnings review), and FY2028 grows in line with revenue.

  • Why EPS holds while revenue is trimmed: Placera's headline of 16-Oct-2026 (web search) reads "Avanza missar förväntningarna – sänker kostnadsprognos" (Avanza misses expectations and lowers its cost forecast); a lower cost guide offsets the revenue trims in the EPS line.
  • The SP against the estimates: the SP fell 14.4% from 28-Aug while FY2027 EPS rose, then rose 13.4% and fell 3.7% with it flat: the estimates explain none of the swings.
  • Test, to the 22-Jan-2027 results: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 26-Oct expects cuts of 2% to 4% to FY2027; none has come. FY2027 EPS below SEK 20.71 (a 2% cut) would be the first.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I understand industry RS sell argument. Is that 10 days similar to the Probing Bets? I think it should be? Check with APM". Under the Position Entry and Exit Rules of 13-Nov-2026 (section 8), the 10-day stop-loss rule covers every stop-loss on a moving average, including, for a Core Investment, the 200-day line rule you asked for in the same note. The six Sell Criteria carry no 10-day window: Industry RS below 30 is read each Friday and sells at the next Tuesday session. A window for it would be a change to the rules, which is yours to make.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts are cutting Sdiptech's FY2026 EPS while raising FY2027 and FY2028. The upgrades are again strongest in revenue and weakest in EPS: more sales, less of each krona reaching earnings.

  • FY2026 EPS: down 8.0% in seven weeks (14.30 at 30-Sep to 13.16), mostly in two steps after the report, (5.4%) on 30-Oct and (1.1%) on 5-Nov, while FY2026 revenue rose 2.9% and EBITDA 3.1%. The cut is below EBITDA: the earnings review attributes 2% of it to the SEK 13m early-redemption charge on the refinanced bonds, with net financial items of SEK (79m) against DNB Carnegie's (70m). DNB Carnegie also trimmed its own 2026 adjusted EPS by 1% on higher segment costs (earnings review); the rest of the 8.0% is not explained in the sources read.
  • Part of the FY2027 and FY2028 EPS rise is financing, not operations: the earnings review records that the lower interest after the refinancing raised 2027 and 2028 adjusted EPS estimates by 5%.
  • FY2027 (year to Dec-27), over 1, 3 and 6 months: EPS +2.1%, +7.9%, +8.5%; revenue +5.2%, +6.8%, +10.7%; EBITDA +2.8%, +4.0%, +7.1%. Since the report (22-Oct), revenue +4.0%, EBITDA +1.8%, EPS +0.5%.
  • FY2028, over 1 and 3 months: EPS +3.7%, +9.2%; revenue +5.9%, +7.5%; EBITDA +3.4%, +4.6%.
  • The trajectory: FY2028 rises slightly faster than FY2027, so analysts are lifting the trajectory a little, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth is 35% from FY2026 to FY2027 (13.16 to 17.79), a jump the FY2026 cut has widened, and 12.3% to FY2028; revenue growth is 8.4% and 7.1%.
  • The implied EBITDA margin: 23.3% for FY2026, 25.1% for FY2027 and 25.2% for FY2028; FY2027's was 25.6% to 22-Oct and 25.3% from the 23-Oct report, and is still slipping.

The links. The pattern matches the report: organic sales of 13% against a forecast of 6%, but organic adjusted EBITA growth of only 6% and a margin miss (earnings review). The SP topped on 5-Nov, the day of the last FY2027 and FY2028 raises, and has fallen 4.9% since with no estimate change; your 3-Nov note: "Almost always, price moves first". Hypothesis: the SP is discounting a slower conversion of sales into EPS than the FY2027 jump implies. Against: FY2027 and FY2028 EPS are still rising, and no analyst has cut revenue. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's FY2027 test is adjusted EPS at or above SEK 16.5: consensus of 17.79 clears it by 7.8%. Test: FY2027 EPS at the 30-Nov month-end; it dipped 0.9% on 30-Oct and recovered on 5-Nov, and a second dip would show the conversion doubt reaching FY2027.

Sdiptech's sell-side picture is positive in direction but has thinned since September (Sell-side Momentum A on 18-Oct, B on 15-Nov); the cut that matters is to FY2026 EPS.

  • Net Upgrade Breadth for FY2027 on 17-Nov: revenue +83% (5 raising of 6), EBITDA +83% (5 of 6), EPS +40% (3 raising and 1 cutting, of 5), PT +43% (3 raising of 7, none cutting).

EPS breadth went from +80% (September month-end) to +33% and +40% after the report. The one analyst cutting FY2027 EPS turned on 23-Oct, the report day; the sources read do not name the broker or give its logic.
  • The divergence: revenue and EBITDA breadth are near their highs while EPS and PT breadth sit at half their peaks. This is the same order as item 4 (Consensus by named FY).

A seventh analyst appears to have joined at a lower PT and without a Buy. The PT count rose from 6 to 7 on 29-Oct. A non-Buy rating appeared on 30-Oct (5 Buys and 1 other, after 24 months of Buys only). Against: the data file's second daily PT series, which carries the 6-to-7 analyst count, shows the average rising from 304.8 to 308.1 on 29-Oct, which would put the new PT near SEK 328; the two series disagree, so the lower-PT reading is not settled. Test: the broker's name and logic, which this week's sources do not give.

Ratings carry no signal; the PT does the work. Analysts rated Sdiptech Buy through its 43% fall to January 2026 and its rise since. The average PT followed the SP, down 35% from 398.8 (August 2024) to 258.4 (January 2026) and up 19% since, to 308.1, now 22.3% above the SP.

The PT-ceiling hypothesis of the 28-Oct note held a fourth time. Sdiptech has turned down each time its SP came within about 12% to 16% of the average PT: 25-May, the August month-end, 14-Oct and now 5-Nov (265.0, with the PT 16.3% above it). The first three times the SP fell 6.6% to 7.8% and analysts then raised the PT; this time the SP has fallen 4.9% so far and the PT has not moved. Test: a PT rise at or after the 30-Nov month-end would repeat the pattern; a flat PT with a falling SP would be the first break in it.

9. Peers

Sdiptech is now trading with the Swedish industrial compounders, not with its cohort. On 17-Nov Sdiptech ((2.9%)) fell with Röko ((3.3%)), OEM International ((2.6%)), Beijer Alma ((2.1%)), Storskogen ((2.0%)) and Beijer Ref ((1.8%)), while the cohort's German member, Indus Holding, rose 3.5%. On 11-Nov the same Swedish group rallied (Storskogen +3.2%, Beijer Ref +2.7%, OEM International +2.5%, Röko +2.0%) and Sdiptech rose only 0.8%. The stocks that track Sdiptech's daily moves most closely are all Swedish (Beijer Ref, Beijer Alma, Munters, OEM International), at weak correlations of 0.24 to 0.26. What it means: Sdiptech is giving more than its share on the group's down days and taking less on its up days, which is how a stock loses Stock RS inside a weak group.

Your four peer checks:

  • SP technicals: the cohort's leaders turned before Sdiptech. Indus Holding's Stock RS fell from 95 a month ago to 80; it is 14.4% below its 52W high and 5.9% below its 50D MA. Storskogen holds up best (Stock RS 64, 2.5% above its 50D MA). Five of the ten other sector peers shown lost more Stock RS over the month than Sdiptech's 13 points (thyssenkrupp 94 to 45, TKH Group 89 to 55, Lifco 47 to 18, Hill & Smith 81 to 62, Aalberts 87 to 70); Storskogen (9), Smiths Group (6) and Rosebank Industries (2) lost less; only Schouw & Co (93) and Röko (62, from 53) gained.

Storskogen (+1%) and Indus Holding (0%) are flat; Lifco and Volati ((4%) each) are being cut. Sdiptech (+8%, +83%) ranks with the leaders on FY2027, not on FY2026 EPS.

What the peers imply for Sdiptech: the sector's money is going to peers with EPS upgrades now. Hypothesis: until FY2026 EPS stops falling or the fourth-quarter report (10-Feb-2027) confirms the FY2027 jump, Sdiptech trades with the weaker Swedish compounders. Test: Sdiptech's move against Röko and Storskogen on the next Swedish up day; matching them would show the 11-Nov lag was a one-off.

  • Finding: the base has lost its higher lows. The 17-Nov close (252.0) is below the 22-Oct low (252.4), and relative price confirmed it; the trend-health tests pass, but three Stage 3 (topping) flags are present.

What the rules say for Helios Towers now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • The advance is well supported, and the support came at the right place: a Retest of the rising absolute 200D MA with a heavy accumulation day (2-Nov), a trigger day through four MAs (4-Nov), and the best up-day volume of its four advances in 12 months (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • The fuel has stopped arriving: consensus was raised 4 to 10-Nov (the 3-Nov step was a seventh analyst returning to the count) and has not moved since; the SP has risen 4.1% since 10-Nov on flat estimates and fading volume, and the average PT is back to 26.1% above the SP, near the gaps at which May's and October's advances stalled (items 4, and 5 and 6).
  • The test is the 245.39p May high, 2.6% above the close after Helios Towers' longest up-run in five years: the volume on which it meets that level decides between a breakout from the base and a third failure at its top (item 1 (SP and MAs)).

What the rules say today: hold. Helios Towers is a Core Investment of four tranches under the six Sell Criteria and the 200-day line rule. The 200-day line rule is clear: the absolute 200D MA 205.99p, its stop-loss 193.63p (the line less 6%), the close 239.0p; no count is running.

The nearest sell tests:

  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70, read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end: Sector RS (35) meets its half; Stock RS (84) is 14 points above 70, from 71 at Fri 6-Nov, 86 at Fri 16-Oct and 63 on 14-Aug. My rough estimate: October's 9.6% fall against a market up 1.8%, about 11% relative, cost 16 points in two weeks, so a relative fall of about 10% by 30-Nov, about 215p at a flat market, would give a sell signal, and the rules would sell the whole holding at the Tue 1-Dec session.
  • The 200-day line rule: a fall of 13.8% to the line starts the 10-day count; a fall of 19.0% reaches the stop-loss, from 16.3% on 10-Nov, 10.9% on 20-Oct and 10.8% on 18-Aug.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, read at 30-Nov: at a flat market a close at or below 195.6p, 18.2% below the close; relative price is 8.4% below its high, from 13.2% a week ago, 17.5% four weeks ago and 21.8% thirteen weeks ago.
  • How they interact: a fall of about 10% gives only the first, at the month-end; 14% to 18% adds the 200-day line count; 18.2% or more at a flat market also gives the relative price sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end; 19% or more reaches the stop-loss, which confirms an exit on that close and sells the whole holding at the next Tuesday session, ahead of the 30-Nov readings.
  • The record: at the 30-Oct month-end Stock RS was 70, one point from the line, and Helios Towers then rose 17.2%.

The next reading days: Fri 20-Nov reads Industry RS below 30 (59 now) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (+1.49%), neither near; Mon 30-Nov reads the three month-end tests above.

Adds: the rules block an add, because Helios Towers holds 8.03% of capital against the 6.68% limit, with four of four tranches.

  • Where Helios Towers stands for an add: the first stage, pulling back or basing; it is basing, at the top of its base since 14-May. The Master Dashboard reads Pulling Back Probable (4 of 4) and Basing Probable (5 of 5), Healthy Retest none (0 of 4) and volatility contraction pattern none (0 of 4). IMCD

Inflecting / S1→S2 Probing Bet · 4.11% of capital in 2 tranches (tranche 1, 2.35%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 2, 1.76%, bought 4-Nov-2026) · readings as at Tue 17-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

IMCD made a new 52W closing high of €106.45 on Mon 16-Nov, rising against a falling market and against its own cohort, and gave back 1.1% to €105.25 on Tue 17-Nov.

  • Over 12 months IMCD is up 41.0% (from €74.62 on 20-Nov-2025); over 3 months, 10.3% (from €95.46 on 20-Aug); over the month, 2.2%; over the week to 17-Nov, 2.1%, against (1.2%) for the Stoxx Europe 600 and (1.9%) for its industry, Industrials and capital goods.
  • IMCD's advance from its 30-Jun low (€79.00) is 33.2% to the 17-Nov close (34.7% to the 16-Nov high); the leg from the 18-Sep low (€93.30) is 12.8% to the 17-Nov close (14.1% to the 16-Nov high).
  • With a beta of 0.47, 2.7% of IMCD's 2.1% week is its own, half its usual weekly move of 5.1%: not a large move, but one against its peers.
  • Your weekly AlphaSense report was not read this week (AlphaSense showed its sign-in page on 18-Nov), so the Developments below come from the earnings review, its implications note (filed 17-Nov) and the data file.

The legs since the 4-Nov purchase of tranche 2, and what explains each:

  • 4 to 9-Nov, €104.50 to €102.25 (2.2%): IMCD's own, on the 5-Nov nine-month results. On 5-Nov IMCD opened 0.7% higher, fell to €100.45 (3.9% below the 4-Nov close) and closed (1.1%) at €103.35. Third-quarter operating EBITA of €140.2m matched consensus exactly and the gross margin (25.1%) beat, but organic revenue growth (5.6% against 6.1% expected) and the conversion margin (42.1% against 42.7%) missed, on higher operating costs, digital investment and a larger commercial headcount (earnings review, 10-Nov, citing Jefferies, Morgan Stanley and Deutsche Bank). Over 4 to 9-Nov Brenntag fell 3.8% and Azelis 1.8%.
  • 9 to 16-Nov, €102.25 to €106.45 (+4.1%): IMCD's own, through a broker downgrade. On Wed 11-Nov Jefferies downgraded IMCD to Hold from Buy and cut its PT to €115 from €122, arguing that inflation is fading and the recovery is priced after a 32% rise this year (implications note, 17-Nov). IMCD opened 1.3% lower that day and closed (0.7%) at €102.30, in the top fifth of the day's range; it then rose 1.6% on 12-Nov and 2.1% on 13-Nov, closing at the day's high. Over 9 to 16-Nov Brenntag fell 5.0% on its own third-quarter statement (in line, no guidance raise, its Specialties margin down 40 basis points, per the implications note), Azelis rose 2.1%, Beijer Ref 5.1% and Bunzl 3.3%. The cause of IMCD's 12 and 13-Nov rise is not in the sources read.
  • 16 to 17-Nov, (1.1%) to €105.25: the market's. The Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.7% and IMCD's industry 1.4%. On 18-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Markets down ~1.5% yesterday (Mon 17-Nov) - Tech worst performer"; the data file dates that fall Tuesday 17-Nov.

The 9 to 16-Nov leg is the week's finding: IMCD rose through a downgrade while Brenntag fell on its own results. Item 9 (Peers) tests what that split means.

The MAs: every MA from the 5D to the absolute 200D MA is rising and stacked in order, and IMCD has closed above its 50D MA at every session since 6-Oct.

  • On 17-Nov: the 20D MA €102.83, the 50D €99.81, the 150D €94.49 and the absolute 200D MA €91.29, each rising over 5 and 20 sessions (the absolute 200D MA up 2.9% over 20).
  • The one MA crossing since 4-Nov was the 20D: IMCD closed below it on 9-Nov (€102.25 against €102.42, on 1.44x the 50D average volume) and back above it on 10-Nov (€103.05, on 0.92x).
  • At IMCD's earlier turns the September pullback broke the 20D and 50D MAs (18-Sep) and the May to June decline broke every MA; since 22-Oct IMCD has traded below its rising 20D MA on 11 sessions and closed below it on one (9-Nov). That is how a rising MA behaves in an advance.
  • Your trend-health tests all pass: the absolute 200D MA rising; the 150D above it; the 50D above both; the SP above every MA, 1.1% below its 52W closing high and 52.8% above its 52W low (€68.87, 19-Mar). The one Stage 3 (topping) red flag present in part is in item 2 (Volume).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

One correction first: the 18-Sep option expiry and index review sits inside IMCD's 50D average volume and lowers each ratio below by about 3%. The ratios leave it out; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure follows in brackets.

IMCD's results were sold on above-average volume for three sessions, and the recovery to a new high came on below-average volume: the reverse of the pattern a healthy breakout shows.

  • 5-Nov (results day): (1.1%) on 1.70x (1.65x), 217,490 shares, €22.5m, closing at 0.61 of the day's range after a 3.9% intraday fall; a distribution day.
  • 6-Nov: (0.6%) on 1.57x (1.52x), 203,067 shares, €20.9m, closing at 0.33 of the range; a distribution day.
  • 9-Nov: (0.5%) on 1.48x (1.44x), 193,100 shares, €19.7m, closing at 0.82 of the range but below the 20D MA; a distribution day.
  • 12, 13 and 16-Nov, the rise to the new high: +1.6% on 0.90x (0.88x), +2.1% on 1.00x (0.98x) and +0.4% on 0.69x (0.67x). No IMCD up day after the 4-Nov accumulation day (1.49x (1.45x)) has reached 1.2x the 50D average.
  • In your Notion note of 10-Nov (last edited 11-Nov) you wrote: "IMCD - Above the 20D MA now. Cleared the SL IMHO. But OBV is negative - concerning; ergo must be on light volume". The volume confirms it: the up days since 10-Nov were light, and the 20-session up/down volume ratio is 0.84 on both bases.

The distribution-day count rose from 1 to 4 in 25 sessions over the week of the results, and has not risen since.

  • The dashboard counted 2 distribution days on 20-Oct, 1 on 3-Nov, 4 on 10-Nov and 4 on 17-Nov: 15-Oct, 5, 6 and 9-Nov. The two accumulation days are 23-Oct and 4-Nov; the 15-Oct distribution day leaves the 25-session window on 19-Nov.
  • On 10-Nov you wrote: "L3D were Distribution Days - why. Azelis + Brenntag similar price movement" and "Only IMCD in its cohort had the higher volume for the price declines; Azelis didn't". The sources read give the why: the 5-Nov conversion margin and revenue misses, then estimate cuts (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). The volume was IMCD's own, as you saw: on 5-Nov Brenntag traded 1.2x and Azelis 0.8x; on 6-Nov Brenntag fell 3.0% on 1.1x and Azelis 1.7% on 0.8x.

Across the whole move, IMCD's buying is the lightest of its 12 months.

  • From the 18-Sep low to the 16-Nov high, IMCD's average volume on up days was 131,454 shares against 148,223 on down days, a ratio of 0.89 (23 up days, 16 down).
  • Over the whole advance from 30-Jun the ratio is 1.11 (the script's basis), against 1.15 in the March to May advance and 1.24 in the January to February advance, and average up-day volume has halved, from 330,827 shares in March to May to 154,208.
  • Hypothesis: the advance reflects an absence of sellers more than strong buying. For it: light up days and a 20-session ratio below 1. Against it: no distribution day since 9-Nov, and the SP made its high while Brenntag was sold on heavy volume (item 9 (Peers)).

One Stage 3 (topping) red flag is present in part: a light-volume breakout. IMCD's 13-Nov close (€106.05) above its 14-Oct high (€104.70) came on 1.00x, and its 16-Nov high on 0.69x. The October breakout (13-Oct, 1.06x) was followed two sessions later by a distribution day on 2.18x (15-Oct) and a 4.3% pullback to the 20D MA. The other red flags are absent: IMCD's largest one-day fall since 30-Jun is still 17-Jul's (4.4%), its largest 5-session fall 15-Sep's (4.2%), and its lows are rising (€93.30 on 18-Sep, €100.15 on 26-Oct, €102.25 on 9-Nov, on closes).

Relative price has not confirmed IMCD's new SP high. On 16-Nov, the day of the SP's 52W closing high, IMCD's relative price was 4.2% below its own 52W high of 4-May; on 17-Nov it is 4.0% below, and 10.3% above its rising relative 200D MA. IMCD's relative price rose on 17-Nov while its SP fell, because the market fell further.

Confirms the breakout: an IMCD close above €106.45 on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more, about 200,000 shares (€21m). Confirms a failed breakout: a close below the 20D MA (€102.83) on 1.5x or more, a repeat of 15-Oct. Fri 20-Nov is an option expiry, so its volume is mechanical and proves neither.

3. RS

IMCD's Stock RS of 82 is the highest reading in the data file's history (from 17-Oct-2025) and its Sector RS of 92 is one point below its 16-Oct high, while Industry RS has read 26 at all five readings since 4-Nov, two of them Fridays: the rotation is inside IMCD's sector, not in its industry.

  • Stock RS: 82 on 17-Nov, from 80 on Fri 13-Nov, 65 on 6-Nov, 77 a month ago (16-Oct) and 57 three months ago (18-Aug). It is 12 points above the line of 70 and 32 above the line of 50.
  • Sector RS (Industrial and chemical distributors and technical trading): 92 on 17-Nov, from 89 on 13-Nov and 76 on 6-Nov; 42 points above its line of 50.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 26 on 4, 6, 12, 13 and 17-Nov, below its line of 30, from 79 on 9-Oct. The Industry Digest's note of 10-Nov records that this reading moves in steps of six to seven points, so one Friday can take it back across 30.

Much of IMCD's Stock RS rise is arithmetic. IMCD's 12-month change rose to +41.0% as last November's results fall (from €82.01 on 11-Nov-2025 to €73.31 on 18-Nov-2025) became the base. IMCD's closes stayed between €71.44 and €79.46 from 17-Nov-2025 to 20-Jan-2026, so the low base lasts to about 20-Jan-2027 and keeps helping Stock RS for two months. The price part is real too: IMCD's SP rose 2.1% in a week when the market fell 1.2%.

The order of the turn: the industry fell first (from 9-Oct), the sector and IMCD followed (from 16-Oct), and the sector and IMCD turned up together on 13-Nov without the industry. Inside the sector the turn is led by stocks outside IMCD's cohort: Beijer Ref's Stock RS is 81 from 54 a month ago and Bunzl's 82 from 75, while Brenntag's fell to 52 from 80 and Azelis's to 75 from 92. IMCD is now the strongest of the three chemical distributors on Stock RS, which it was not a month ago (Azelis 92, Brenntag 80, IMCD 77).

Precedent: the last time IMCD's Stock RS turned down near a high (61 at the April month-end, before the 4-May top), it fell to 15 by June and the SP fell 21.9%. Test: Stock RS on Fri 20-Nov, with the base effect still in its favour.

4. Consensus by named FY

IMCD's estimates rose for one day on the results and were cut back below their pre-results level within a week: FY2027 and FY2028 EBITDA and revenue are now lower than before 5-Nov, and EPS is flat.

  • Over 1, 3 and 6 months (to 17-Nov): FY2026 EPS (€5.638) +0.1%, +1.7%, +5.8%; FY2027 EPS (€6.075) +0.1%, +0.9%, +5.3%; FY2028 EPS (€6.612) (0.2%), +0.7%, +5.5%.
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 revenue (€5,383m) (0.4%), (0.1%), +2.8% and FY2027 EBITDA (€613.5m) (1.1%), (0.6%), +5.0%; FY2028 revenue (€5,652m) (0.6%), (0.2%), +2.6% and EBITDA (€649.5m) (1.2%), (0.6%), +5.4%. FY2026 EBITDA (€585.1m) is (0.7%) over the month.
  • The results week, daily: FY2027 EBITDA rose to €625.5m on 5-Nov and fell to €613.5m by 17-Nov, (1.9%); FY2028 EBITDA from €662.7m to €649.5m, (2.0%); FY2027 revenue from €5,432m to €5,383m, (0.9%). FY2027 EPS went from €6.109 to €6.075, (0.6%). The data file shows the cuts on 6, 9, 10 and 16-Nov (on 16-Nov FY2027 EBITDA went from €615.6m to €613.5m and FY2027 revenue from €5,396m to €5,383m); the implications note (17-Nov) dates Jefferies' downgrade and estimate cuts 11-Nov.

The cuts since 5-Nov are about equal across the three years (EBITDA (1.8%) for FY2026, (1.9%) for FY2027, (2.0%) for FY2028), so analysts are lowering the level, not pulling earnings forward or reshaping the trajectory. Implied growth from FY2027 to FY2028 is 8.8% for EPS, 5.9% for EBITDA and 5.0% for revenue; from FY2026 to FY2027, 7.8%, 4.9% and 3.9%. The implied EBITDA margin rises only from 11.3% (FY2026) to 11.4% (FY2027) and 11.5% (FY2028): analysts see little operating leverage, which fits the conversion margin miss and the cost growth the implications note puts at about 11% in the third quarter.

The SP has led the estimates by a wide margin. IMCD's SP rose 10.3% over 3 months while FY2027 EPS rose 0.9% and FY2027 EBITDA fell 0.6%. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Over 6 months IMCD passes it on EPS (+5.3% for FY2027); over 3 months, on EBITDA and revenue, it does not.

The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's tests: the implications note (17-Nov) judges the results "negative" and "material", because Sell-side Momentum moved from B to C on one house's downgrade and cuts; the case's one price trip, a close below the 50D MA, was not triggered. The conversion margin (42.1%) sits at the bottom of the case's range (42% to 44%), and Jefferies' finding that customers' inventory days are 6% above a year ago challenges the case's view that channel inventories are at mid-cycle. Test: the full-year results on 19-Feb-2027 (confirmed), the next date that can settle conversion and costs.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

IMCD lost two Buys in the week after its results, and the one broker who has given a reason gave valuation, not the quarter.

  • Ratings (Buy / other / Sell): 11 / 3 / 1 at the October month-end; 11 / 2 / 1 on 6-Nov; 10 / 2 / 1 on 9-Nov, as one Buy left the count; 9 / 4 / 1 on 10-Nov. That is 9 Buys of 14 analysts, from 14 Buys at the September and October 2025 month-ends.
  • Who and why: Jefferies downgraded IMCD to Hold from Buy on 11-Nov and cut its PT to €115 from €122, arguing that inflation is fading and the recovery is priced after a 32% rise this year (implications note, 17-Nov). Morgan Stanley stayed Equal-weight with a €110 PT and Deutsche Bank stayed Buy with €123 (same source). The second Buy lost (9-Nov) is not named in the sources read.

Hypothesis: IMCD's analysts move their ratings with the gap to their PTs, so Buys fall as the SP closes on the PT. For it: IMCD's Buys peaked at 14 when the average PT stood 65% (September 2025 month-end) and 56% (October 2025) above the SP; they fell to 9 at the April 2026 month-end, with the gap at 12.2%; they are 9 again now, with the gap at 11.1%; and Jefferies' stated reason is valuation. Against it: Buys rose from 10 to 11 in July 2026 while the SP rose, after the 29-Jul results. What follows: more downgrades are likely if the SP rises without PT raises.

IMCD's average PT (€116.9) is 11.1% above the SP, the narrowest gap at any month-end since at least October 2024. The last time it was this narrow, 12.2% at the April 2026 month-end, IMCD's SP topped on 4-May and fell 21.9% to 30-Jun. The average PT was flat at €116.0 from 29-Sep to 4-Nov, touched €118.6 on 6 and 9-Nov and was back at €116.9 by 11-Nov, after Jefferies' cut, with the number of analysts in the average between 13 and 15. The PT is not following the SP up; the SP is closing on it.

Net Upgrade Breadth rose after the results while the consensus levels fell: more analysts raised than cut, but the cuts were larger.

  • On 17-Nov, against the October month-end: EPS +29% (6 raised, 2 cut, of 14) from +7%; EBITDA +17% (4 and 2, of 12) from 0%; revenue +15% (4 and 2, of 13) from 0%; PT +14% (3 and 1, of 14) from +7%. The extra revenue and PT cuts came on 10-Nov.
  • Where analysts are cutting: Jefferies is the one cutter named (rating, PT and estimates, 11-Nov); its logic is that inflation is fading, the recovery is priced, and customers' inventory days are 6% above a year ago, so future volume has to come from demand rather than restocking (implications note). The other cutters are not named in the sources read. The data file grades IMCD's Sell-side Momentum C, from B a month ago (18-Oct).

Precedent: IMCD's PT breadth recovered after its last results (from +13% at the July month-end to +47% at August's); this time the results have come and gone, and the next scheduled test is the 19-Feb-2027 full-year results.

9. Peers

IMCD has split from Brenntag and now trades closer to Azelis: of the three chemical distributors, IMCD alone closes above its 20D and 50D MAs.

  • Brenntag: (6.0%) over the week and (10.2%) over the month; below its 20D and 50D MAs and 0.5% above its absolute 200D MA; its 11-Nov third-quarter statement fell 3.5% on 2.2x the 50D average volume, then 3.3% on 2.8x on 12-Nov, closing at the day's low. IMCD rose 1.6% on 12-Nov.
  • Azelis: +0.2% over the week and (11.4%) over the month; below its 20D and 50D MAs; its fall from the 14-Oct high followed its own third quarter on 22-Oct (free cash flow down 52.5%, leverage 3.46 times, a €350m goodwill impairment, per the Sector Digest's note of 28-Oct).
  • The correlation: IMCD's daily moves track Brenntag's at 0.64 over 63 sessions but 0.27 over the last 10; Azelis's at 0.62 over 63 and 0.72 over 10. The Sector Digest's hypothesis of 28-Oct, that the market pays for cash conversion and balance sheet in this group, has passed both tests it named: IMCD's 5-Nov and Brenntag's 11-Nov reports.

Your four peer checks, for the two chemical distributors:

  • Sell-side estimates: Brenntag's 3-month EPS revision is (4%) with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth at 0%; Azelis's is +4% with revenue breadth at +36%. IMCD sits between them (+1%, +15%), as it did a month ago.
  • SP technicals: Brenntag is the weakest, Stock RS 52 from 80 a month ago and 1.9x volume again on 17-Nov; Azelis's Stock RS is 75 from 92, on light volume (0.33x on its 2.1% fall on 17-Nov).
  • Newsflow: Brenntag's third quarter was in line, with no guidance raise, its Specialties margin down 40 basis points, and a new platform aimed at smaller and mid-sized accounts (implications note, 17-Nov), the customers the Sector Digest's note of 13-Oct says producers are handing to specialty distributors such as IMCD. IMCD's EMEA organic gross profit grew 8.3% against Azelis's 2.1%; its Americas grew 3.4% against Azelis's 14.9% (same source).
  • Company guidance and communications: IMCD gave its first directional outlook (positive organic operating EBITA growth for FY2026, a "promising start" to the fourth quarter) and expects no goodwill write-down; Brenntag did not raise its guidance; Azelis took the €350m impairment.

Outside the cohort, the sector's strength comes from other distributors: Beijer Ref (+2.2% over the week, Stock RS 81 from 54 a month ago) and Bunzl (Stock RS 82), while Diploma fell 3.0% (Stock RS 64 from 84).

What the peers imply for IMCD: Hypothesis one, better placed on this week's evidence: IMCD is bought as the quality chemical distributor while Brenntag is sold, on IMCD's gross margin beat and 2.6 times leverage. Hypothesis two: IMCD follows its cohort's turns down with a lag; against it, in October IMCD and Azelis made their closing highs on the same day (14-Oct) and turned together, and Azelis fell 13.4% to 26-Oct while IMCD fell 4.3%; for it, the 63-session correlation is still strong and IMCD's up days are light (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)). Test: IMCD's reaction to Brenntag's next heavy-volume down day; a close below IMCD's 20D MA on such a day would point to the second.

What the rules say for IMCD now, and what decides the next step

4. Consensus by named FY

FY2027 is CVS Group's year to June 2027; FactSet consensus in sterling; no change since 13-Nov.

CVS Group's consensus is edging up and the furthest year rises fastest, but by little: the SP has re-rated on almost unchanged numbers.

  • EPS: FY2027 95.6p, +1.1% in a month (from the 30-Sep month-end), flat over 3 months; FY2028 104.1p, +0.9% and (0.7%); FY2029 113.1p, +1.6% and (3.4%).
  • Revenue: FY2027 £751.0m, FY2028 £785.3m and FY2029 £823.8m, +0.4%, +0.6% and +2.1% in a month; EBITDA £150.8m, £159.1m and £167.5m, +0.3%, +0.6% and +1.1%, FY2029 still (3.7%) over 6 months.
  • Implied growth: EPS 8.8% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 8.6% to FY2029; revenue 4.6% and 4.9%; implied EBITDA margin 20.1%, 20.3% and 20.3%, near the bottom of the company's 19% to 23% target range.
  • From the 1-Oct trough to 17-Nov, FY2027 EPS rose 1.2% (94.5p to 95.6p) while the SP rose 19.5% (1,159.9p to 1,386p).

The test is the four-month trading update with the annual general meeting on 25-Nov. UK like-for-like growth slowed to about 1.5% in the second half of FY2026 from 2.7% in the first (earnings review, 24-Sep). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): "A November update that reports group LFL above 2.5 percent (which would mean practice LFL near 2 percent) would move the FY2027 number toward 96p and the breadth of revisions to neutral four months early." With consensus already at 95.6p, that outcome would mainly improve EPS breadth, now (10%). Refutes: a figure near the second half's 1.5%, which would leave a 19.5% re-rating with no estimate support.

4. Consensus by named FY

Since the 29-Oct statement Carlsberg's consensus has risen about 0.9% for FY2027 and FY2028 on every line, but revenue and EBITDA rose by the same amount at an unchanged margin, the mark of a currency translation gain rather than an operating upgrade; and since 10-Nov the rise has stopped.

  • Since the statement (28-Oct to 17-Nov): FY2026 EPS +0.3% (DKK 64.92 to DKK 65.12); FY2027 EPS +0.85% (DKK 69.72 to DKK 70.31); FY2028 EPS +0.96% (DKK 76.13 to DKK 76.86). FY2027 revenue +0.93% and EBITDA +0.86%; FY2028 revenue +0.97% and EBITDA +0.91%.
  • Since 10-Nov: flat; FY2027 EPS (0.2%), DKK 70.44 to DKK 70.31.
  • Over the chart's periods: FY2027 EPS +0.7% over 1 month, (0.3%) over 3 and (0.7%) over 6; FY2028 EPS +1.0%, +0.7%, +0.2% and (3.4%) over 12 months; FY2027 EBITDA is still 1.1% below 3 months ago.
  • The trajectory: FY2028 has risen slightly faster than FY2027 since the statement, so analysts nudged the trajectory up rather than pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth is 8.0% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 9.3% from FY2027 to FY2028.
  • The margin, and why currency: the implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) is 21.4%, unchanged since 28-Oct and below 21.8% on 18-Aug; FY2028's is 21.7%, unchanged. The statement raised the translation tailwind to DKK 100m; Deutsche Bank raised FY2027 and FY2028 EPS 2.5% on currency and Bernstein FY2027 EPS 1% (earnings review), while Rothschild & Co Redburn cut FY2027 organic sales growth to 3.0%.
  • The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): it judges that "The scope for upgrades over the coming 6-12 months is nil at EPS, negative at revenue, positive at the share count". The three weeks since fit that once currency is set aside. Its dated tests are the FY2027 guidance at the 9-Feb-2027 results (it expects a midpoint of 4-5%, below consensus EBIT growth) and Chongqing Brewery's fourth-quarter volume no worse than (5%).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule #J". On levels Carlsberg does not meet it: FY2027 EPS is (0.3%) over 3 months and (0.7%) over 6. On breadth it has met it only since 29-Oct (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)).   • Coverage: up over 12 months, from 23 to 25 analysts with a PT.

9. Peers

In September and October Carlsberg turned first and led the brewers; since 5-Nov AB InBev, Heineken and Diageo have made new 52W highs and Carlsberg has not: leadership in the group has passed to Heineken.

  • The SP technicals: AB InBev made a 52W closing high on 13-Nov and is 1.2% below it; Heineken and Diageo made theirs on 16-Nov and are 0.9% and 1.3% below. Carlsberg is 2.4% below its 5-Nov high.
  • Who turned first: Carlsberg, in the autumn (on 1-Oct it held its 18-Sep low while Heineken and AB InBev made lower lows). In November Heineken's Stock RS rose 23 points over the month to 79 and AB InBev's is 80, against Carlsberg's 76.
  • The sell-side estimates: Carlsberg has the best estimate breadth of its cohort: revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +60% against Heineken +15%, Royal Unibrew +12% and AB InBev (20%). Its 3-month EPS revision (0%) matches Heineken's and beats AB InBev's (1%). So the peers that are leading on price are not leading on estimates: the brewers' November move is a rotation trade, and within it Carlsberg's better breadth has not been rewarded.
  • The newsflow and company guidance: the third-quarter statements split along China. On 29-Oct the market sold the brewers that kept guidance without raising it, AB InBev (3.2%) and Carlsberg (3.0%), while Heineken, which grew in China, held (Assistant Portfolio Manager note, 30-Oct). Heineken's 28-Oct update showed organic revenue +4.6% and Asia Pacific volume +17.3% (note of 28-Oct). China's third-quarter beer volumes fell 9.6% at Chongqing Brewery (Carlsberg's), 9% at Tsingtao and 16.8% at Budweiser APAC (AB InBev's) (note of 30-Oct). This week's peers newsflow was not read.
  • Which group Carlsberg trades with: its daily moves track Heineken and Royal Unibrew most closely (average correlation 0.71 each), then AB InBev (0.67) and Diageo (0.56). Royal Unibrew, listed in Copenhagen like Carlsberg, fell with it from 5 to 11-Nov ((3.1%)) but then rose 5.5% to 16-Nov while Carlsberg rose 1.3%; so the lag is not a Danish flow.
  • What the peers imply for Carlsberg: the group's strength keeps Beverages' Sector RS (88) and Consumer staples' Industry RS (92) far from their lines, so the Sector RS and Industry RS tests are not where Carlsberg's risk lies. The risk is relative: if Carlsberg keeps lagging brewers that are rising, its relative price falls, and that brings forward the test of its relative 200D MA (closing paragraph). Test: Carlsberg against Heineken and AB InBev over the next two Fridays; a Carlsberg close above DKK 1,031.5 with the brewers would restore its leadership.
